Listed below are all the Standard decks that earned 21 points or more in the Constructed rounds of Pro Tour *Khans of Tarkir*. Each deck is preceded by a short explanation of how the deck works. 

**Jeskai Wins** 

Using a significant compliment of burn spells and hard-hitting creatures, Jeskai Wins often ends the game in a flurry of damage. It's also capable of adapting a controlling role on occasion if the situation calls for it.

#### Marco Cammilluzzi

**Temur Monsters** 

The deck uses mana acceleration to quickly pump out some of the biggest, scariest creatures in the format.

#### Pedro Carvalho

**Jeskai Wins** 

Using a significant compliment of burn spells and hard-hitting creatures, Jeskai Wins often ends the game in a flurry of damage. It's also capable of adapting a controlling role on occasion if the situation calls for it.

#### Justin Cheung

**Blue-Black Control** 

A pure control strategy, this two-color deck wants to remove all of the opponent's threats one-by-one, pulling ahead on cards with [Dig Through Time](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dig+Through+Time) and [Jace's Ingenuity](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%27s+Ingenuity). [Perilous Vault](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Perilous+Vault) is the sweeper of choice, and can reset virtually any board to the control player's favor.

**Mardu Planeswalkers** 

This midrange deck generates most of its value from its Planeswalkers, then uses a suite of removal and disruption to keep the opponent off-balance while the Planeswalkers do their work.

**Abzan Midrange** 

This [Sylvan Caryatid](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sylvan+Caryatid)/[Courser of Kruphix](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Courser+of+Kruphix) deck is packed full of value and individually powerful spells, trying to jam many of the format's best cards into the same 75-card decklist.

**Blue-Black Control** 

A pure control strategy, this two-color deck wants to remove all of the opponent's threats one-by-one, pulling ahead on cards with [Dig Through Time](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dig+Through+Time) and [Jace's Ingenuity](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%27s+Ingenuity). 

#### Ivan Floch

**Jeskai Ascendancy Combo** 

The format's only true combo deck, [Jeskai Ascendancy](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jeskai+Ascendancy) uses its namesake card along with creatures that make mana to churn through its deck, casting spell after spell before ultimately killing with a large attacking creature, decking from [Altar of the Brood](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Altar+of+the+Brood), or any number of ways. [Retraction Helix](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Retraction+Helix) and a zero-cost spell make the combo repeatable as many times as necessary. 

#### Eric Froehlich

**Jeskai Wins** 

Using a significant compliment of burn spells and hard-hitting creatures, Jeskai Wins often ends the game in a flurry of damage. It's also capable of adapting a controlling role on occasion if the situation calls for it.

#### Francesco Giorgio

**Abzan Midrange** 

This [Sylvan Caryatid](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sylvan+Caryatid)/[Courser of Kruphix](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Courser+of+Kruphix) deck is packed full of value and individually powerful spells, trying to jam many of the format's best cards into the same 75-card decklist. 

#### Danny Goldstein

#####

**Mardu Midrange** 

This midrange deck generates most of its value from its Planeswalkers, then uses a suite of removal and disruption to keep the opponent off-balance while the Planeswalkers and efficient, frightening creatures do their work.

#### Nathan Herkimer

#####

**Abzan Midrange** 

This [Sylvan Caryatid](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sylvan+Caryatid)/[Courser of Kruphix](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Courser+of+Kruphix) deck is packed full of value and individually powerful spells, trying to jam many of the format's best cards into the same 75-card decklist. 

#### Jonathan Hickerson

#####

**Red-White Heroic** 

Brutally fast and efficient, this deck utilizes the heroic mechanic to turn small, fast red and white creatures into large, fast red and white creatures.

#### Yoshihiko Ikawa

**Jeskai Wins** 

Using a significant compliment of burn spells and hard-hitting creatures, Jeskai Wins often ends the game in a flurry of damage. It's also capable of adapting a controlling role on occasion if the situation calls for it.

**Naya Midrange** 

This midrange deck generates most of its value from its Planeswalkers, accelerating them out with green mana creatures like [Sylvan Caryatid](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sylvan+Caryatid). Some of the format's strongest creatures let the deck also go big.

**Abzan Midrange** 

This [Sylvan Caryatid](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sylvan+Caryatid)/[Courser of Kruphix](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Courser+of+Kruphix) deck is packed full of value and individually powerful spells, trying to jam many of the format's best cards into the same 75-card decklist.

**Jeskai Wins** 

Using a significant compliment of burn spells and hard-hitting creatures, Jeskai Wins often ends the game in a flurry of damage. It's also capable of adapting a controlling role on occasion if the situation calls for it.

**Abzan Midrange** 

This [Sylvan Caryatid](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sylvan+Caryatid)/[Courser of Kruphix](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Courser+of+Kruphix) deck is packed full of value and individually powerful spells, trying to jam many of the format's best cards into the same 75-card decklist.

**Abzan Midrange** 

This [Sylvan Caryatid](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sylvan+Caryatid)/[Courser of Kruphix](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Courser+of+Kruphix) deck is packed full of value and individually powerful spells, trying to jam many of the format's best cards into the same 75-card decklist. 

#### Ari Lax

#####

**Abzan Midrange** 

This [Sylvan Caryatid](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sylvan+Caryatid)/[Courser of Kruphix](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Courser+of+Kruphix) deck is packed full of value and individually powerful spells, trying to jam many of the format's best cards into the same 75-card decklist. 

#### Aaron Lewis

#####

**Abzan Aggro** 

This more aggressive take on Abzan often forgoes [Sylvan Caryatid](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sylvan+Caryatid) and [Courser of Kruphix](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Courser+of+Kruphix) for more aggressively-minded creatures, such as [Rakshasa Deathdealer](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakshasa+Deathdealer) and [Herald of Torment](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Herald+of+Torment), trying to put opponents off balance with some of the strongest creatures at every point in the curve. 

#### Josh McClain

**Jeskai Wins** 

Using a significant compliment of burn spells and hard-hitting creatures, Jeskai Wins often ends the game in a flurry of damage. It's also capable of adapting a controlling role on occasion if the situation calls for it.

#### Andrea Mengucci

**Unwritten Devotion** 

This green and black devotion deck utilizes [Nykthos, Shrine to Nyx](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nykthos%2C+Shrine+to+Nyx) to make a ton of mana, supports a constellation sub-theme, AND goes for the big score with [See the Unwritten](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=See+the+Unwritten).

**Red-White Tokens** 

[Hordeling Outburst](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hordeling+Outburst) and [Goblin Rabblemaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Rabblemaster) team up with [Monastery Swiftspear](https://web.archive.org/web/20141012000000/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Monastery+Swiftspear) and a large helping of burn to quickly drop the opponent to zero.
